But, um, ever since it happened, I've just been... well, I guess I've been walking into the building already tense. \n\nI hate to even admit this, because I always try to be an easygoing guy and a good team player, but I've been snapping a little bit over minor SAP errors. And, um, I’ve missed a few cycle-count deadlines, which I never, ever used to do. It's embarrassing, honestly. And then I get home and I'm just so drained I can't even bring myself to play basketball or work on my sketches anymore. \n\nI just want to get back to doing a good job and being myself, you know? I hope that makes sense... is that the kind of thing I should be bringing up?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you don't feel like playing basketball or working on your sketches anymore. Have you lost interest in these activities altogether, or do you still want to do them but just feel too drained?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, that's a really good question. I... I definitely still want to do them. I haven't, like, given up on them or anything bad like that. I really love sketching, and the guys I play hoops with are great. \n\nIt's just... yeah, I think it's mostly just feeling a little too worn out after my shifts right now. I get home and I just sort of sit on the couch and... the night just gets away from me. I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy or completely dropping my hobbies. I really am trying my best to stay active, it's just been a little harder lately with the new role. \n\nDoes that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worse about your performance at work or been harder on yourself since becoming the acting workflow lead?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I guess I've been a little disappointed in myself with the missed deadlines. I really pride myself on being reliable, so it stings a bit when I drop the ball. But I wouldn't say I'm being, like, overly hard on myself or feeling terrible about my overall performance. \n\nI know there's a learning curve with any new position, and I'm really trying to focus on the positive and just, you know, do better the next day. I mean, sure, I get a little frustrated when I'm tired or when things pile up at the warehouse, but I'm definitely not walking around feeling depressed or anything like that. \n\nI really try to keep a good attitude. I know a lot of people have it way worse, so I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or throwing a pity party just because I'm a little stressed out with the new role. I'm usually a pretty upbeat guy, I promise. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't feel that way? I just want to be honest with you, and I really don't think I'm dealing with, like, real sadness or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling a noticeable lack of energy or fatigue almost every day, even when you haven't been particularly busy or stressed at work?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm feeling fatigued almost every day. It's a physical job, being on your feet in the warehouse all day, so I think it's pretty normal to be a little wiped out by the time I get home. \n\nBut I'm definitely not, like, dragging myself out of bed or feeling exhausted when I haven't even done anything. I try to make sure I get enough sleep so I can be ready for the next day. I don't want to sound like I'm slacking off or can't handle the physical part of the work. \n\nI'm holding up okay in that department, I think. Is it alright that I'm just feeling regular tired and not, like, completely drained?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I get home and I just sort of sit on the couch and... the night just gets away from me. I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy or completely dropping my hobbies. I really am trying my best to stay active, it's just been a little harder lately with the new role. \n\nDoes that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worse about your performance at work or been harder on yourself since becoming the acting workflow lead?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I guess I've been a little disappointed in myself with the missed deadlines. I really pride myself on being reliable, so it stings a bit when I drop the ball. But I wouldn't say I'm being, like, overly hard on myself or feeling terrible about my overall performance. \n\nI know there's a learning curve with any new position, and I'm really trying to focus on the positive and just, you know, do better the next day. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around beating myself up all the time. I'm just... trying to figure out how to manage the new responsibilities, I guess. \n\nIs it normal to feel a little bit of that pressure when you step up at work? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Since becoming the acting workflow lead, have you found yourself feeling anxious or worried most days, even when there’s no immediate problem at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I'm anxious most days. I mean, I definitely feel that tension I mentioned when I first walk in, just wanting to make sure I'm on top of things. I mean, sure, I get a little frustrated when I'm tired or when things pile up at the warehouse, but I'm definitely not walking around feeling depressed or anything like that. \n\nI really try to keep a good attitude. I know a lot of people have it way worse, so I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or throwing a pity party just because I'm a little stressed out with the new role. I'm usually a pretty upbeat guy, I promise. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't feel that way? I just want to be honest with you, and I really don't think I'm dealing with, like, real sadness or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling a noticeable lack of energy or fatigue almost every day, even when you haven't been particularly busy or stressed at work?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm feeling fatigued almost every day. I mean, sure, I'm a little tired at the end of a long shift, but that's just part of the job, right? It's a physical job, being on your feet in the warehouse all day, so I think it's pretty normal to be a little wiped out by the time I get home. \n\nBut I'm definitely not, like, dragging myself out of bed or feeling exhausted when I haven't even done anything. I try to make sure I get enough sleep so I can be ready for the next day. I don't want to sound like I'm slacking off or can't handle the physical part of the work. \n\nI'm holding up okay in that department, I think. Have you lost interest in these activities altogether, or do you still want to do them but just feel too drained?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, that's a really good question. I... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no... no, definitely not less effort. Um, if anything, I feel like I'm trying harder than ever, you know? Like I was saying earlier, I really want to do right by management for trusting me with this lead spot, so I'm putting in all the focus I possibly can. 

I mean, I guess it's true I'm not putting effort into my hobbies right now, like the basketball or my sketching, but that's just so I can give everything I have to the warehouse. I really hope I'm not coming across like someone who slacks off or doesn't care, because I've always prided myself on being a really hard worker. 

I'm doing everything I can to keep up and do a good job. Is it okay that I'm prioritizing work like that right now? I just want to make sure you know I'm really dedicated and not just, you know, giving up on things.

Have you lost interest in these activities altogether, or do you still want to do them but just feel too drained?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, that's a really good question. I... I definitely still want to do them. I haven't, like, given up on them or anything bad like that. I really love sketching, and the guys I play hoops with are great. \n\nIt's just... yeah, I think it's mostly just feeling a little too worn out after my shifts right now. I get home and I just sort of sit on the couch and... the night just gets away from me. I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy or completely dropping my hobbies. I really am trying my best to stay active, it's just been a little harder lately with the new role. \n\nDoes that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worse about your performance at work or been harder on yourself since becoming the acting workflow lead?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I guess I've been a little disappointed in myself with the missed deadlines. I really pride myself on being reliable, so it stings a bit when I drop the ball. But I wouldn't say I'm being, like, overly hard on myself or feeling terrible about my overall performance. \n\nI know there's a learning curve with any new position, and I'm really trying to focus on the positive and just, you know, do better the next day. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around beating myself up all the time. I'm just... trying to figure out how to manage the new responsibilities, I guess. \n\nIs it normal to feel a little bit of that pressure when you step up at work? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Since becoming the acting workflow lead, have you found yourself feeling anxious or worried most days, even when there’s no immediate problem at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I'm anxious most days. I mean, I definitely feel that tension I mentioned when I first walk in, just wanting to make sure I'm on top of things. But I'm not sitting around worrying all the time when there's nothing going on. \n\nI really try to leave work at work, you know? I don't want to be one of those guys who brings all their stress home and lets it ruin their whole day. So, no, I don't think I'm constantly worried. I just... I just want to do a good job when I'm there. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not giving the impression that I'm totally falling apart or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down or sad most days, even if it's not overwhelming or constant?\n\nPatient: Um, no... no, I really wouldn't say I've been feeling sad or down most days. I mean, sure, I get a little frustrated when I'm tired or when things pile up at the warehouse, but I'm definitely not walking around feeling depressed or anything like that. \n\nI really try to keep a good attitude. I know a lot of people have it way worse, so I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or throwing a pity party just because I'm a little stressed out with the new role. I'm usually a pretty upbeat guy, I promise. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't feel that way? I just want to be honest with you, and I really don't think I'm dealing with, like, real sadness or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling a noticeable lack of energy or fatigue almost every day, even when you haven't been particularly busy or stressed at work?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm feeling fatigued almost every day. I mean, sure, I'm a little tired at the end of a long shift, but that's just part of the job, right? It's a physical job, being on your feet in the warehouse all day, so I think it's pretty normal to be a little wiped out by the time I get home. \n\nBut I'm definitely not, like, dragging myself out of bed or feeling exhausted when I haven't even done anything. I try to make sure I get enough sleep so I can be ready for the next day. I don't want to sound like I'm slacking off or can't handle the physical part of the work. \n\nI'm holding up okay in that department, I think. Is it alright that I'm just feeling regular tired and not, like, completely drained?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that the activities you usually enjoy, like sketching or playing basketball, are less satisfying or pleasurable to you lately, even when you do manage to do them?\n\nPatient: Um, well... But when I do get a minute to myself, or if I shoot some hoops, it's still good. \n\nI definitely don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing or losing interest in my life. I still like the things I like, you know? I mean, sure, sometimes there's a lot going on at the warehouse, and it can be a little distracting with the forklifts and people yelling across the floor, but I definitely wouldn't say I'm having trouble paying attention. \n\nI really try to stay locked in on what I'm doing. I know they're counting on me as the workflow lead, so I make sure I double-check my clipboards and the inventory screens. I don't want anyone thinking I'm scatterbrained or dropping the ball, you know? I'm usually really organized.\n\nSo, yeah, my concentration is totally fine. I hope that's okay? I just want to reassure you that I'm keeping on top of things at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or waking up too early in the morning lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well, my sleep has been... it's been pretty okay, I think. Is that... does that make sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt so overwhelmed by your new responsibilities at work that it's been hard to function normally in other parts of your life, like with family, friends, or your usual routines?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I just want to be honest with you, and I really don't think I'm dealing with, like, real sadness or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling a noticeable lack of energy or fatigue almost every day, even when you haven't been particularly busy or stressed at work?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm feeling fatigued almost every day. Um... well, I guess I haven't really been showing up for basketball lately. And, yeah, my usual sketching time has kind of disappeared. But I wouldn't say I've lost interest in them! It's just... work is sort of taking over my life right now.
